❯ /plan                                                                         
  ⎿  Current Plan
     /home/jeremy/.claude/plans/eventual-inventing-chipmunk.md

     Identity Sled: Cozo persistence + native btrfs device add

     Context

     The identity_sled plugin (landed in da92efbb) is the core container-identity
     surface: the container IS the sled IS the identity, host = container zero,
     persistence = a Cozo-registered btrfs device attached via btrfs device add.
     Two proposed gaps remain from the handoff
     (docs/handoff-2026-07-09-identity-sled-netmaker.md, OD-34):

     1. No durable store — identity_sled_dispatch.rs keeps sleds + session events
     only in the MutationEngine state cache (capped at 256 events); a restart of
     opdbus loses everything. The handoff claimed sessions/session_memories
     relations existed in op-cozo-store — they don't; they must be added.
     2. btrfs device_add is a stub (OD-32) — SledBtrfsDevice.attached is never
     actually actioned; the btrfs plugin is schema-only with no execution
     anywhere.

     Also: the deployed /usr/local/bin/opdbus binary (Jul 8 19:03) predates the 
     identity_sled dispatch — live zcall identity_sled … cannot work until op-web
     is rebuilt and redeployed. The sealed blob
     identity_sled.19eea502b72c52c2.blob IS live in the catalog, so the
     plugin/schema side exists.

     Already staged in the working tree (uncommitted)

     - crates/op-cozo-store/src/lib.rs:
       - New relations in seed_schema: identity_sleds (full sled row,
     btrfs_device_json as pre-serialized JSON) and session_events (session_id, 
     seq => kind, subid, content, created_at).
       - New structs IdentitySledRecord, SessionEventRecord; helpers
     row_to_identity_sled.
       - New API (mirrors the WgSessionRecord pattern from PR #15):
     put_identity_sled, get_identity_sled, list_identity_sleds,
     touch_identity_sled (partial-column :update), append_session_event (seq
     allocated inside one multi_transaction write), list_session_events (newest
     first, :order -seq :limit).
     - crates/op-network/src/btrfs.rs (NEW): native BTRFS_IOC_ADD_DEV ioctl
     (0x5000940A, compile-time size assert on btrfs_ioctl_vol_args = 4096) — pub 
     fn device_add(device, mount_point). No subprocess, per the no-Command::new
     rule; op-network is the native-OS-ops crate.
       - Still needs: pub mod btrfs; added to crates/op-network/src/lib.rs (edit
     was blocked by plan mode).

     Remaining implementation

     1. crates/op-network/src/lib.rs

     Add pub mod btrfs; to the module list.

     2. crates/op-plugins/src/state_plugins/identity_sled.rs

     - Add mount_point: String field to SledBtrfsDevice (the btrfs fs the device
     is added TO — required by the ioctl; #[serde(default)]).
     - Add method attach_btrfs_device to the schema surface: input { session_id 
     }, output = updated identity; SideEffect::Mutation, capability
     identity_sled.write, subid mut.service.identity-sled.btrfs-device.attach@v1
     (format-validated by the existing all_subids_are_valid test; the registry is
     a runtime plugin, not a hardcoded list).

     3. crates/op-grpc-bridge — dispatch wiring (identity_sled_dispatch.rs)

     - Cargo.toml: add op-cozo-store path dep (opdbus already links it
     transitively via op-web; no new heavy dep).
     - Cozo handle: lazy static SLED_COZO: OnceLock<Option<CozoGraphShuttle>>,
     opened at first dispatch from IDENTITY_SLED_COZO_DB_PATH env, default
     /var/lib/op-dbus/identity-cozo. Own path — never the users-cozo or 
     cognitive-mcp path (sled engine is single-process; op-web-server and
     op-cognitive-mcp already hold those locks). Open failure → warn once, run
     cache-only (current behavior preserved; tests stay hermetic).
     - All Cozo calls via tokio::task::spawn_blocking (sync sled I/O off the
     async runtime — same pattern as PR #15 wireguard_auth).
     - Hydration: tokio::sync::OnceCell — on first dispatch,
     list_identity_sleds() warms the cache if it's empty (one read at startup;
     not a watcher/poll — reactive rule intact).
     - write_identity → after cache write, put_identity_sled (failure = warn!,
     mutation still succeeds: the event chain is the durable proof, Cozo is the
     queryable archive — comment already in the file says exactly this).
     - touch_session → touch_identity_sled(sid, now, true).
     - record_session_event → seq from append_session_event when Cozo is up
     (durable monotonic across restarts), fallback to the cache-derived seq; keep
     the capped in-state ledger as-is.
     - get_session_history → serve from list_session_events when Cozo is up;
     fallback to cache.
     - New attach_btrfs_device arm: look up the sled's btrfs_device; error if
     absent or already attached; run op_network::btrfs::device_add(device_path, 
     mount_point) via spawn_blocking; on success set attached = true, bump
     mutation_index, persist to cache + Cozo, return the identity. Explicit
     mutation only — never implicit in write_identity (attaching claims/wipes a
     block device).

     4. Verify + deploy

     - cargo check -p op-cozo-store -p op-network -p op-plugins -p op-grpc-bridge
     -p op-web
     - cargo test -p op-plugins identity_sled (schema surface + subid validation)
     and a new op-cozo-store in-memory round-trip test for the sled + event API.
     - cargo build --release -p op-web, deploy opdbus to /usr/local/bin, restart
     via s6d (service name to confirm on host), then live-verify:
       - zcall identity_sled get_identity → should now dispatch (error "no
     identity sled" is OK — proves the arm exists)
       - zcall identity_sled write_identity --arguments 
     '{"wireguard_pubkey":"<hub-peer-key>"}' → row lands in
     /var/lib/op-dbus/identity-cozo
       - restart opdbus → get_identity still returns the sled (hydration proof)
       - zcall shared_unix_socket get_config — should stop returning "Missing
     Ghostbridge Identity Sled" once container-zero's identity is written (per
     handoff step 3)
     - SIGNALS.md: append OD-34 progress row. Commit on
     claude/refine-local-plan-0xcmyp.

     Explicitly out of scope (per handoff, need user decisions)

     - Identity-source re-architecture (xray-injected header off the VPS hop vs
     local WG) — open design decision.
     - Blob encryption (chacha20poly1305) and AnnaScribe migration off the raw
     mmap.
     - EMQX exhook socket wiring, netmaker-first-boot.

● User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/eventual-inventing-chipmunk.md · /plan to 
     edit
     Identity Sled: inherit the Incus schema, Cozo persistence, native btrfs 
     device add

     Context

     The identity_sled plugin (landed in da92efbb) is the core
     container-identity surface: the container IS the sled IS the identity, host
     = container zero, persistence = a Cozo-registered btrfs device attached
     via btrfs device add. Two proposed gaps remain from the handoff
     (docs/handoff-2026-07-09-identity-sled-netmaker.md, OD-34):

     1. No durable store — identity_sled_dispatch.rs keeps sleds + session
     events only in the MutationEngine state cache (capped at 256 events); a
     restart of opdbus loses everything. The handoff claimed
     sessions/session_memories relations existed in op-cozo-store — they don't;
     they must be added.
     2. btrfs device_add is a stub (OD-32) — SledBtrfsDevice.attached is never
     actually actioned; the btrfs plugin is schema-only with no execution
     anywhere.

     Also: the deployed /usr/local/bin/opdbus binary (Jul 8 19:03) predates the 
     identity_sled dispatch — live zcall identity_sled … cannot work until
     op-web is rebuilt and redeployed. The sealed blob
     identity_sled.19eea502b72c52c2.blob IS live in the catalog, so the
     plugin/schema side exists.

     Already staged in the working tree (uncommitted)

     - crates/op-cozo-store/src/lib.rs:
       - New relations in seed_schema: identity_sleds (full sled row,
     btrfs_device_json as pre-serialized JSON) and session_events (session_id, 
     seq => kind, subid, content, created_at).
       - New structs IdentitySledRecord, SessionEventRecord; helpers
     row_to_identity_sled.
       - New API (mirrors the WgSessionRecord pattern from PR #15):
     put_identity_sled, get_identity_sled, list_identity_sleds,
     touch_identity_sled (partial-column :update), append_session_event (seq
     allocated inside one multi_transaction write), list_session_events (newest
     first, :order -seq :limit).
     - crates/op-network/src/btrfs.rs (NEW): native BTRFS_IOC_ADD_DEV ioctl
     (0x5000940A, compile-time size assert on btrfs_ioctl_vol_args = 4096) — pub
     fn device_add(device, mount_point). No subprocess, per the no-Command::new
     rule; op-network is the native-OS-ops crate.
       - Still needs: pub mod btrfs; added to crates/op-network/src/lib.rs (edit
     was blocked by plan mode).

     Remaining implementation

     1. crates/op-network/src/lib.rs

     Add pub mod btrfs; to the module list.

     2. crates/op-plugins/src/state_plugins/identity_sled.rs — inherit the Incus
     schema

     User direction: the identity sled inherits the Incus schema and creating a 
     container = creating an identity sled. The typed IncusInstance (modeled on
     official shared/api Instance/InstancePut, typed NamedDevice union) already
     exists in state_plugins/incus.rs in the same crate — reuse it, don't
     redefine.

     - Add instance: Option<IncusInstance> to ContainerIdentitySled
     (#[serde(default)], subid src.service.identity-sled.instance@v1) — the
     container's full Incus definition lives IN the sled; IncusInstance already
     derives JsonSchema so it embeds cleanly.
     - Add mount_point: String field to SledBtrfsDevice (the btrfs fs the device
     is added TO — required by the ioctl; #[serde(default)]).
     - New method provision_container: input { wireguard_pubkey, psk?, 
     blob_ref?, btrfs_device?, instance? }, output = the written identity.
     Semantics:
       - session_id is DERIVED (Argon2 via op_identity::session), never
     supplied; the instance name is forced to session_id — the container name IS
     the identity.
       - instance supplies image/profiles/config/devices overrides; nic-type 
     devices are rejected (hard rule: no container NIC/IP — all I/O over the UDS
     surface).
       - subid mut.service.identity-sled.container.provision@v1.
     - New method attach_btrfs_device: input { session_id }, output = updated
     identity; subid mut.service.identity-sled.btrfs-device.attach@v1. (Subids
     are format-validated by the existing all_subids_are_valid test; the
     registry is a runtime plugin, not a hardcoded list.)
     - Expose the incus create path for reuse: make IncusPlugin::apply_create
     pub(crate)→pub (it already does real creation over the Incus UDS API at
     /var/lib/incus/unix.socket + run_incus_command, then syncs
     profiles/config/devices/status).

     3. crates/op-grpc-bridge — dispatch wiring (identity_sled_dispatch.rs)

     - Cargo.toml: add op-cozo-store path dep (opdbus already links it
     transitively via op-web; no new heavy dep).
     - Cozo handle: lazy static SLED_COZO: OnceLock<Option<CozoGraphShuttle>>,
     opened at first dispatch from IDENTITY_SLED_COZO_DB_PATH env, default
     /var/lib/op-dbus/identity-cozo. Own path — never the users-cozo or 
     cognitive-mcp path (sled engine is single-process; op-web-server and
     op-cognitive-mcp already hold those locks). Open failure → warn once, run
     cache-only (current behavior preserved; tests stay hermetic).
     - All Cozo calls via tokio::task::spawn_blocking (sync sled I/O off the
     async runtime — same pattern as PR #15 wireguard_auth).
     - Hydration: tokio::sync::OnceCell — on first dispatch,
     list_identity_sleds() warms the cache if it's empty (one read at startup;
     not a watcher/poll — reactive rule intact).
     - write_identity → after cache write, put_identity_sled (failure = warn!,
     mutation still succeeds: the event chain is the durable proof, Cozo is the
     queryable archive — comment already in the file says exactly this).
     - touch_session → touch_identity_sled(sid, now, true).
     - record_session_event → seq from append_session_event when Cozo is up
     (durable monotonic across restarts), fallback to the cache-derived seq;
     keep the capped in-state ledger as-is.
     - get_session_history → serve from list_session_events when Cozo is up;
     fallback to cache.
     - New attach_btrfs_device arm: look up the sled's btrfs_device; error if
     absent or already attached; run op_network::btrfs::device_add(device_path, 
     mount_point) via spawn_blocking; on success set attached = true, bump
     mutation_index, persist to cache + Cozo, return the identity. Explicit
     mutation only — never implicit in write_identity (attaching claims/wipes a
     block device).
     - New provision_container arm: derive session_id (reuse the write_identity
     derivation, PSK-aware); force instance.name = session_id; reject nic
     devices; call IncusPlugin::apply_create(&instance); on success write the
     sled (with the instance embedded) to cache + Cozo — provisioning IS writing
     the sled. Incus create failure = the mutation fails, no sled row (a sled
     exists ⟺ its container exists, mirroring blob-⟺-plugin).
     - Cozo column: add instance_json: String default "" to the staged
     identity_sleds relation (+ IdentitySledRecord.instance_json, row-helper
     index update) so the embedded instance persists.

     4. Verify + deploy

     - cargo check -p op-cozo-store -p op-network -p op-plugins -p 
     op-grpc-bridge -p op-web
     - cargo test -p op-plugins identity_sled (schema surface + subid
     validation) and a new op-cozo-store in-memory round-trip test for the sled
     + event API.
     - cargo build --release -p op-web, deploy opdbus to /usr/local/bin, restart
     via s6d (service name to confirm on host), then live-verify:
       - zcall identity_sled get_identity → should now dispatch (error "no
     identity sled" is OK — proves the arm exists)
       - zcall identity_sled write_identity --arguments 
     '{"wireguard_pubkey":"<hub-peer-key>"}' → row lands in
     /var/lib/op-dbus/identity-cozo
       - restart opdbus → get_identity still returns the sled (hydration proof)
       - zcall shared_unix_socket get_config — should stop returning "Missing
     Ghostbridge Identity Sled" once container-zero's identity is written (per
     handoff step 3)
     - SIGNALS.md: append OD-34 progress row. Commit on
     claude/refine-local-plan-0xcmyp.
     - Optional live provision test (only if user wants it this session): zcall 
     identity_sled provision_container --arguments '{"wireguard_pubkey":"<test-k
     ey>","instance":{"image":"images:alpine/3.20"}}' → container appears in
     incus list named by the derived session_id, sled row lands in Cozo.

     Explicitly out of scope (per handoff, need user decisions)

     - Deprovision path (delete_container = remove sled + delete instance) —
     follow-up once provision is proven.
     - Seed-blob → rootfs assembly for provision (image source is still an Incus
     image ref; the sealed-blob-as-rootfs pipeline is the netmaker-appliance
     spec work).
     - Identity-source re-architecture (xray-injected header off the VPS hop vs
     local WG) — open design decision.
     - Blob encryption (chacha20poly1305) and AnnaScribe migration off the raw
     mmap.
     - EMQX exhook socket wiring, netmaker-first-boot.

          +.clone()),

      148 +/// One-time cache warm from Cozo: after an opdbus restart the
          + state cache is                                               
      149 +/// empty; the persisted sleds are its restart-warm source. On
          +e read at first                                               
      150 +/// dispatch — not a watcher, not a poll.                     
      151 +async fn ensure_hydrated(engine: &MutationEngine) {           
      152 +    static HYDRATED: OnceCell<()> = OnceCell::const_new();    
      153 +    HYDRATED                                                  
      154 +        .get_or_init(|| async {                               
      155 +            let Some(cozo) = sled_cozo() else { return };     
      156 +            let cozo = cozo.clone();                          
      157 +            let rows = tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || coz
          +o.list_identity_sleds()).await;                               
      158 +            let rows = match rows {                           
      159 +                Ok(Ok(rows)) => rows,                         
      160 +                Ok(Err(e)) => {                               
      161 +                    tracing::warn!(error = %e, "identity sled 
          +hydration read failed");                                      
      162 +                    return;                                   
      163 +                }                                             
      164 +                Err(e) => {                                   
      165 +                    tracing::warn!(error = %e, "identity sled 
          +hydration task failed");                                      
      166 +                    return;                                   
      167 +                }                                             
      168 +            };                                                
      169 +            if rows.is_empty() {                              
      170 +                return;                                       
      171 +            }                                                 
      172 +            let mut cache = read_cache(engine).await;         
      173 +            if !cache.sleds.is_empty() {                      
      174 +                return;                                       
      175 +            }                                                 
      176 +            cache.sleds = rows.iter().map(record_to_sled).coll
          +ect();                                                        
      177 +            cache.sleds.sort_by(|a, b| a.session_id.cmp(&b.ses
          +sion_id));                                                    
      178 +            if let Err(e) = write_cache(engine, &cache).await 
          +{                                                             
      179 +                tracing::warn!(error = %e, "identity sled hydr
          +ation cache write failed");                                   
      180 +            }                                                 
      181 +        })                                                    
      182 +        .await;                                               
      183 +}                                                             
      184 +                                                              
      185 +/// Persist one sled row to Cozo; failure is logged, not fatal
          + — the event                                                  
      186 +/// chain already notarized the mutation.                     
      187 +async fn persist_sled(sled: &ContainerIdentitySled) {         
      188 +    let Some(cozo) = sled_cozo() else { return };             
      189 +    let cozo = cozo.clone();                                  
      190 +    let rec = sled_to_record(sled);                           
      191 +    match tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || cozo.put_identit
          +y_sled(&rec)).await {                                         
      192 +        Ok(Ok(())) => {}                                      
      193 +        Ok(Err(e)) => tracing::warn!(error = %e, "identity sle
          +d Cozo persist failed"),                                      
      194 +        Err(e) => tracing::warn!(error = %e, "identity sled Co
          +zo persist task failed"),                                     
      195 +    }                                                         
      196 +}

  Committed as d8159487 on claude/refine-local-plan-0xcmyp — all plan items
  implemented and verified at the unit level:

  - Cozo durability: new identity_sleds + session_events relations in
  op-cozo-store with a typed API; round-trip tests pass (including a Cozo quirk
  I hit: max() over an empty relation yields a Null row, which would have made
  the first event seq 1 instead of 0 — fixed and covered by test).
  - Incus schema inheritance: ContainerIdentitySled now embeds instance: 
  Option<IncusInstance> (official shared/api shape), and the new
  provision_container method creates the container via IncusPlugin::apply_create
  with the name forced to the derived session_id — container creation IS sled
  creation, and nic devices are rejected outright.
  - Native btrfs attach: op_network::btrfs::device_add issues BTRFS_IOC_ADD_DEV
  directly (no subprocess), exposed as the explicit attach_btrfs_device
  mutation; SledBtrfsDevice gained mount_point.
  - Dispatch: opens its own Cozo store at /var/lib/op-dbus/identity-cozo
  (env-overridable), all I/O under spawn_blocking, one-time cache hydration on
  first dispatch so a restart no longer loses sleds.

  Two op-plugins test failures (software schema drift, subid gate on
  fail2ban/persona/etc.) reproduce on the clean tree — pre-existing, not from
  this change. One naming trap confirmed for deploy: the s6 service running the
  gRPC binary is op-dbus; the opdbus service is actually the WG tunnel.

  Next, when the build lands: install to /usr/local/bin/opdbus, s6d restart 
  op-dbus, then live-verify zcall identity_sled get_identity / write_identity
  and the restart-hydration path.

  Three traps surfaced along the way (committed to SIGNALS.md and my memory):

  1. The first "verification" was a lie: the s6 service execs
  /usr/local/bin/op-dbus (hyphen) — the Jul-8 deploy had only updated opdbus,
  which nothing runs. And the old binary's generic path returns success: true
  with your args echoed back, which looks like a working call.
  2. Routing bug (fixed in 95d41d89): zcall/PluginService.CallMethod goes
  through MutationEngine::mutate, which had no identity_sled arm — only the
  typed-service path did. Any future plugin dispatch needs wiring in both.
  3. Bootstrap chicken-and-egg: the gate drops all calls without
  /dev/shm/plugin_schema.dat, including the call that would create identity.
  Seeded via WG_PUBKEY=<hub-peer-key> op-identity-sled --refresh.

  One cosmetic issue left noted in SIGNALS: zcall renders integer fields (seq,
  mutation_index, created_at) as null — the simd→prost Value conversion drops
  ints in CallMethodResponse; the projection JSON has correct values.
  provision_container and attach_btrfs_device are deployed but not yet exercised
  against a real container/device — say the word and I'll run a live provision
  test (alpine image, throwaway key).

  body["profiles"] = … assumes the key is present, but it was never inserted

  the shared incus plugin — never exercised live before because nothing had
  called apply_create with profiles set (or --no-profiles) through this code
  path. Fixing it properly with as_object_mut().insert(...):

● This is Incus daemon-level, not application code — every attempt fails
  identically at the SQLite/dqlite projects table. Checking scope:

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

❯ threr is supposed  to be zero sql in this project.. where is sql?             

● That SQL is not ours — it's Incus's own internal database. Incus (the
  third-party container hypervisor we call via its Unix-socket REST API) uses
  dqlite (distributed SQLite) internally to track its own
  instances/projects/profiles tables — that's how upstream Incus is built,
  independent of anything in this repo. The "no SQL for state" rule in CLAUDE.md
  governs our application state (op-dbus/op-cozo-store, which correctly uses
  CozoDB, not SQLite) — it was never meant to reach into Incus's own internals,
  which we only talk to as a client over its REST API.

  So the context canceled ... "projects" table error is Incus's own dqlite
  backend failing on a write, not a SQL violation in our code. That's a
  legitimate Incus daemon health issue on this host (separate from the code bugs
  I already fixed) — worth investigating, but it's an infrastructure problem,
  not a code-review finding.

  Want me to keep digging into why Incus's dqlite is stuck (e.g. check incus 
  admin sql global/daemon logs), or pause the live provisioning test here since
  the identity_sled code path itself is now proven correct up to the point of
  calling Incus?

  Two things confirm it's not Cozo:
  1. "projects" is an Incus concept, not one of ours — it's Incus's own
  project/namespace table (incus project list works fine for reads). Our Cozo
  relations are named identity_sleds, session_events, wg_sessions, etc. — no
  overlap.
  2. The direct socat probe never touched op-dbus or Cozo at all — it's a raw
  HTTP request straight to /var/lib/incus/unix.socket. The error originates
  entirely inside the incusd daemon's own dqlite (distributed SQLite) backend,
  which is how upstream Incus persists its own state — a separate process,
  separate database, separate socket from anything we built.

  So this is Incus's own daemon-health issue on this host, independent of the
  identity_sled work. Given the SIGNALS.md history of recovery interventions on
  this box (stale storage pools, orphaned test-launch rows), it's plausible the
  dqlite store is still in a degraded state from that. Want me to keep digging

● Good insight — this might be a socat artifact, not an Incus daemon issue.
  socat's stdin pipe closes (EOF) right after printf finishes, and it
  half-closes the write side of the UDS connection — Go's net/http server can
  interpret that as the client disconnecting and cancel the request context
  mid-flight. Our actual Rust client (incus_api_request) uses a persistent
  tokio::net::UnixStream and never closes it early — I never actually retested
  our code path after the error-formatting fix, only the socat probe. Let's
  check that directly:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Two real bugs found, using a properly-behaved persistent socket:

  1. incus_api_call doesn't recognize async responses — Incus returns 202 
  Accepted with "status":"Operation created" for instance creation, but the code
  only matches literal "success"/"created"/"accepted", so it falls into the
  error-extraction branch and reports the empty top-level "error":"" field
  instead of polling the operation.
  2. The actual failure Incus reports (buried in the operation's metadata):
  "Image not provided for instance creation" — because our code sends alias: 
  "images:ubuntu/24.04" verbatim. images: is a CLI-only remote shorthand; the
  REST API's source.alias expects just "ubuntu/24.04" (optionally with
  server/protocol for a remote, or nothing extra for an already-cached local
  image).

  Both are pre-existing latent bugs in the shared incus plugin, never previously
  exercised. Fixing them:

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

           sn't already

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Root cause fully identified — this was never Cozo or an Incus daemon-health
  issue. Two real bugs in the shared Incus plugin's REST client:

  1. It never handled Incus's async 202 Accepted/operation responses — it read
  the envelope's own (empty) error field instead of polling
  /operations/<id>/wait for the real result.
  2. It sent the CLI-style images:ubuntu/24.04 shorthand verbatim as the REST
  alias field, which Incus rejects ("Image not provided for instance creation")
  since the bare API only understands the alias itself.

  Also fixed the 500ms idle-read heuristic, which would have falsely truncated
  the new long-poll wait call. Rebuild in progress — will deploy and do the full
  end-to-end provision test once it lands.

● The failure is now hitting different steps each time (create, profile-add)
  with the same empty-response symptom, but Python probes in between always
  succeed cleanly — this is a transient connection race under rapid connection
  churn against Incus's Unix-socket HTTP server, not a deterministic bug. The
  principled fix: an empty (0-byte) response is never valid HTTP, so retry the
  request rather than treat it as a real answer.
